  folio 155) — **eleven men sharing the element, and none of them an archive principal figure.**
- **"Zinda Pil" is not "Zinda Pir".** Shaikh Ahmad of Jam is "known as Zinda Pil (Elephant Colossus)"
  and died "in Muharram 536/August, 1141" at Jam (p. 155, folio 137); `darbar-ghamkol-sharif-zinda-pir`'s
  principal figure is Khwaja Muhammad Qasim (Zinda Pir), d. 1999. **Two words, two men, eight
  centuries.**
- **"Rukn, the Mahdi claimant"** — "Before Saiyid Muhammad Ashraf's arrival in India, a holyman named
  Rukn claimed to be the Mahdi. It was during the reign of Sultan Firuz Shah, who duly had Rukn
  beheaded" (p. 289, folio 269). **A one-word name that collides with an archive row's short form.
  This man is not a saint of the archive and is not mapped.**
- **"Shaikh Kamal alias Shaikh Kalu"**, who "died at Kara, about forty-one miles west of Allahabad"
  (p. 284, folio 264) — **a name collision only; no archive row is reached.**
- **"Shaikh Sulaiman"**, a disciple of ʿAbduʾl-Quddus (p. 357, folio 335), **"Badru'd-Din Sulaiman"**,
  a son of Baba Farid (p. 170, folio 152), and **"Srimat Sulaiman"**, an Arakanese minister (p. 395,
  folio 371) — **three bare Sulaimans, none of them
  `darbar-hazrat-khawaja-shah-muhammad-sulaiman-taunsvi-r-a`'s Khwaja Muhammad Sulaiman Taunsvi
  (d. 1850) and none of them `purana-bhalwal`'s Hazrat Shah Sulaiman Nuri.**
- **"Shaikh Taqi" and "Tahir"** — two Shaikh Taqis (p. 398, folio 374) and one Tahir, the father of
  Shaikh Hasan Tahir who "migrated from Multan" (p. 285, folio 265). **Neither is
  `tahir-bandagi-qadri`'s Hazrat Tahir Bandagi Qadri (d. 1630); the book's Tahir is a
  fifteenth-century migrant to Bihar.**
- **"Shaikh Nizamuʾd-Din" — the Auliyaʾ of Delhi, Nizamuʾd-Din Kharitadar the embezzling treasurer
  (p. 156, folio 138), Shaikh Nizamuʾd-Din Abuʾl-Muyʾid (p. 139, folio 121), Nizamuʾd-Din Yamani the
  hagiographer (p. 21, folio 7), Maulana Nizam Madani (p. 249, folio 229) and Nizamuʾd-Din Ahmad the
  historian (p. 407, folio 383)** — the book distinguishes the first two explicitly; none is an
  archive principal figure.
- **"Shaikh Badruʾd-Din"** — the bare form at p. 244 (folio 224), "Shaikh Badru'd-Din became a victim
  of this power struggle as has already been mentioned", **is named without further identification
  and collides with Khwaja Badruʾd-Din Samarqandi, Shaikh Badruʾd-Din of Ghazna, Maulana Badruʾd-Din
  Ishaq and Pir Badr-i ʿAlam. The book does not say which.**
- **"Shaikh Zahid"** — the index carries "Shaikh Zahid, 352" and "Zahid, Shaikh, son of Rafgatu’d-Din,
  260" as **two separate headwords for what may or may not be two men** (pp. 485, 491; folios 461,
  467), cited to the PDF pages the index is printed on.
- **"Shaikh Sadruʾd-Din"** — the Multan khalifa (pp. 220–226, folios 202–206) and "Shaikh Sadruʾd-Din
  of Quniya" (pp. 225, 290; folios 205, 270) are **not distinguished by any further name in the
  book**; a third, Shaikh Sadruʾd-Din Hakim of Delhi (p. 267, folio 247), a fourth, Shaikh
  Sadruʾd-Din of Meerut (p. 430, folio 406), a fifth, Sadruʾd-Din Ibrahim the Shiʿi sufi (p. 269,
  folio 249), a sixth, Sadruʾd-Din al-Qunawi (p. 122, folio 104), a seventh, Maulana Sadruʾd-Din the
  cousin (p. 195, folio 177) and an eighth, Maulana Sadruʾd-Din the deputy qazi (p. 260, folio 240),
  plus Rinchana's Muslim name Sadruʾd-Din (p. 312, folio 290) and Saiyid Sadruʾd-Din = Raju Qattal
  (p. 301, folio 279).
- **One structural feature of the index a searcher must know** — "About seventy-five consecutive
  headwords across pp. 484–485 (folios 460–461) begin with the word `Shaikh ` and are alphabetised on
  the *given name* that follows it — `Shaikh ‘Abdu'l-‘Aziz Makki, 304` through `Shaikh as-Sajjada,
  102`. **The index is inconsistent about this: the same book indexes other men by given name with
  the title inverted after it** — `Shamsu’d-Din Turk, Shaikh, 270`, `Zahid, Shaikh, son of
  Rafgatu’d-Din, 260`, `Zainu'd-Din ‘Ali, Shaikh, 186-87 .`, `Ya‘qub, Shaikh, 150` (pp. 485, 491;
  folios 461, 467)." **A researcher who searches one form will miss the other.** The same doubling
  affects place-names: "Gorakhtari, 389" and "Gorkhattari, 389" carry the same single folio "in two
  spellings the index does not cross-reference to each other" (p. 466, folio 442), and "Shiva,
  331-34, 337, 349-50, 355, 365, 368" stands beside "Siva, 109" as **two headwords with no
  cross-reference between them** (p. 486, folio 462).

### K. Negative findings — what this part of the book does not give

- **Malik Ayaz is not named anywhere in the book** — stated flatly at p. 128 (folio 110), and Mahmud
  of Ghazna "is not the principal figure of any archive row" (p. 83, folio 67). **That is the whole
  of this book's bearing on `darbar-malik-ahmad-ayaz`, and it is a zero.**
- **Guru Nanak's sister is not named.** She is implied by "brother-in-law" at p. 409 (folio 385) and
  nowhere named. **`gurdwara-babay-nanki` gains nothing from this book — (not stated).**
- **Not one of the archive's named Sants has an index headword** in the range the Pass 1 workers
  could read: not Thawan Das, Gurdas Ram, Bhagat Ram, Asudaram, Satramdas, Kanwar Ram, Vilayat Rai,
  Dharmdas or Bankhandi (p. 484, folio 460).
- **The Naushahia and the Azeemia silsilas are absent** from the index ranges covered (pp. 457–491,
  folios not stated–467); only Naqshbandiyya, Qadiriyya, Chishtiyya, Kubrawiyya, Firdausiyya,
  Suhrawardiyya, Shattariyya and the Qalandari/Madari/Jalali groups can be reported.
- **Nine of the twenty-eight chunks yield no archive-location toponym at all** — chunks 002, 003,
  004, 006, 016, 017 (but for Multan and Uch), 021 (but for Peshawar), 023 (but for Gorkhattri) and
  026 report explicitly that reversing the toponym convention would gain the archive nothing in their
  range (pp. 30–49, 50–65, 66–81, 100–117, 265–280, 281–296, 349–365, 383–401, 442–456; folios
  16–33, 34–49, 50–65, 84–99, 245–260, 261–276, 327–343, 361–377, 418–432).
- **No archive row's ʿurs date appears anywhere in this material.** The only two dated observances in
  the whole of my part of the book are Salar Masʿud's at Bahraich (p. 336, folio 314) and Saifuʾd-Din
  Bakharzi's at Bukhara (p. 247, folio 227), and neither is an archive site.
- **No coordinate, no Auqaf reference, no modern visitor figure and no modern custodian appears
  anywhere in this material** — the book's horizon stops in the early nineteenth century and its
  latest named authority is Ghulam Sarwar's *Khazinatuʾl-Asfiyaʾ* of 1281/1864-65 (p. 29, folio 15).
- **Several sentences in my source are cut off at a chunk boundary and cannot be completed from this
  material** — the ʿAyyub-and-Qazi story (p. 29, folio 15); Khwaja Muʿinuʾd-Din's sentence at p. 133
  (folio 115); Nizamuʾd-Din's death sentence at p. 180 (folio 162) and its continuation at p. 181
  (folio 163); Maulana Husamuʾd-Din's Qutuʾl-Qulub sentence (p. 197, folio 179); the Abu Tawwama
  paragraph (p. 248, folio 228); Gisu Daraz's Hindawi sentence (p. 348, folio 326); Kabir on Gorakh
  (p. 401, folio 377); and Ibn Battuta's Amroha quotation (p. 331, folio 309). **None has been
  completed and none should be.**

## Citable passages

*Fifteen, selected from the hundred and thirty candidates the chunk notes carry, and chosen for use
inside a shrine entry rather than for eloquence: each carries a date, a name, a place, an
institution or a claim that an entry would want to quote. Page references are exactly as the source
notes give them. Every damage flag is kept: the printed form comes first, the reading follows in
square brackets, and nothing flagged has been silently repaired. This is an English-language book
and the notes record no Urdu, Persian or Punjabi script anywhere in the citable material, so there
is no original script to reproduce. **No index or endnote locator has been turned into a page
reference; the two index-derived datings below are cited to the PDF page the index entry is printed
on and are labelled as index evidence.***

- "Generally, Shaikhs were buried inside their khangahs [OCR?] [khanqahs] or close-by." (p. 121,
  folio 103) — **the book's general rule on where a shrine comes from, and the one sentence that
  licenses reading a khanqah site as a future tomb site.**
- "Eventually rulers or important nobles, devoted to a particular Shaikh, erected imposing tombs on
  the graves of their patron saints." (p. 121, folio 103) — the companion rule on who builds the
  building.
- "the ʿurs or wedding, for the death of a mystic denoted the return of his soul to the supernatural
  source" (p. 121, folio 103) — **the book's definition of ʿurs, usable in any entry that has to
  gloss the word.**
- "Later Muslims posthumously conferred on Shaikh Hujwiri the title, Data Ganj Bakhsh, 'Distributor
  of (Unlimited) Treasure'." (p. 130, folio 112) — the origin and gloss of the title, in the book's
  own words.
- "From there he reached Lahore and stayed near the tombs of Shaikh Husain Zanjani and Shaikh 'Ali
  Hujwiri." (p. 139, folio 121) — **the one sentence in this book that names two Lahore shrines
  together and fixes their proximity.**
- "the Baba is believed to have died on 5 Muharram 664/17 October 1265" (p. 174, folio 156, footnote
  9) — a death date given as a dual AH/CE pair, with the book's own hedge "is believed to" kept.
- "Ulugh Khan himself visited Shaikh Farid and offered a farman of four villages to the Shaikh and a
  cash payment for dervishes." (p. 162, folio 144) — **an endowment with a named donor, a named
  instrument and a stated size.**
- "made him the head of a khangah [OCR?] [khanqah] in Siwistan (Sehwan) known as the Khanqah-i
  Muhammadi to which several villages were attached" (p. 299, folio 277) — **a named khanqah at
  Sehwan with a named endowment; the book's only institutional sentence on that place.**
- "Often he wore a red dress, hence his name lal (red). Shaikh Baha'u'd-Din Zakariyya gave him the
  title 'Shahbaz,' the noblest species of falcon." (p. 328, folio 306) — **the origin of both halves
  of the name, with the title's giver named.**
- "even his tomb in Sind is known for innumerable miraculous feats" (p. 328, folio 306) — the
  book's one sentence on that tomb's reputation, and it names no town.
- "the flags are taken to the tomb on the first Sunday of the Hindi month Jaith (May-June)" (p. 336,
  folio 314) — **a festival calendar fixed to a Hindu month for a Muslim shrine; the only such
  sentence in the book.**
- "The Buddhist and Hindu Magh people settled some villages as endowment for the shrine and made it
  a centre of pilgrimage." (p. 339, folio 317) — **a named non-Muslim community endowing a Muslim
  shrine with villages.**
- "At the same time, large amounts of money are paid to those who tend the shrine." (p. 140, folio
  122) — custodianship as an income, stated without a figure.
- "Akbar ruled against the Shaikh, and transferred the administration of the shrine to Shaikh
  Muhammad Bukhari, a trusted officer" (p. 144, folio 126) — **a custodianship changed by imperial
  decision, with both parties named.**
- "Female mystics were never incorporated into khanqahs and orders as spiritual succession could not
  be traced through them." (p. 427, folio 403) — **the book's flat structural claim about women and
  succession, which any entry on a woman's shrine will want to quote and argue with.**

*Two further passages are worth having and are given separately because they rest on index evidence
alone: the index's own dating "Baba Farid (b. 1173-74 or 1175-76, d. 1265)" and "Baha'u'd-Din
Zakariyya, Shaikh (b. 1182-83, d. 1262)", both printed on p. 461 (folio 437). **These are index
headwords. The locators inside them have not been converted into page references, and the birth
alternatives in the first are the index's own and are not resolved.***

## Doubts and review points

### 1. Route, flags and what the marks in this file are

**This book is on the `tesseract` route and its source carries NO transcriber apparatus at all — no
`[OCR?]`, no `[illegible]` and no `[folio N]` line exists in the transcription.** All twenty-eight chunks
state this independently. **Every `[OCR?]` mark in this file was placed by a Pass 1 reader on the exact
damaged word, printed form first and the reading in square brackets, and every one has been carried onto
the word it was attached to.** The measured totals for the book are **779 `[OCR?]`, 9 `[text-layer?]` and
36 `[illegible]` across 28 chunks**, with **6,042 page references (pp. 1–491) and 6,106 folio
references**; the `[text-layer?]` count is an artefact of the audit, because this route produces no
text-layer marks and none appears in the notes. **Nothing in this file has been silently repaired.** Where
a reading could not be settled, the printed form stands alone and the fact is marked **(not stated)**.

### 2. The damage classes, inventoried once (convention (b): flagged inline above AND listed here)

1. **`q` read as `g` — the dominant class, and it lands on exactly the vocabulary this archive lives on.**
   Measured over 704 flagged pairs that carry an explicit reading, the leading cases are **`khangah` →
   *khanqah* (39 occurrences)** and **`khirga` → *khirqa* (14)**, with `Figh` → *Fiqh*, `Tughlug` /
   `Tughlugq` → *Tughluq*, `Tariga` → *Tariqa*, `mugta'` → *muqta'*, `gawwal` → *qawwal*, `galandar` →
   *qalandar*, `fagir` → *faqir*, `Hagiqa` → *Haqiqa*, `Tabagat` → *Tabaqat*, `Managib` → *Manaqib*,
   `Samargand` → *Samarqand*, `si/sila`-adjacent forms and dozens of title-words. **It is systematic but
   NOT reversible token by token, because a real `g` exists in the book's own transliteration, so the full
   inline treatment stands and no g-form has been normalised.** The decisive proof that these are OCR and
   not the book's spelling is inside one line of the bibliography: "al-Tabagat al-Kubra or Lawagih
   al-anwar fi tabaqat al-Akhyar" (p. 447, folio 423) **prints the same word corrupt and clean four words
   apart**; the same pages print "Qushairi", "Qazi", "Qutb", "Maqbul" and "Tabaqat" correctly.
2. **Italic `l` read as `/`, `1`, `J`, `A`, `t`, `i` or lost — the second-heaviest class, and it destroys
   the archive's institutional vocabulary.** `/angar` → *langar* (**every langar in chunks 011, 014, 018,
   025 is damaged, including the ʿurs passage and the founding grant of the first Kashmir khanqah**),
   `si/sila` → *silsila*, `sil/silas` → *silsilas*, `/b/is` → *Iblis*, `s/okas` → *slokas*, `khi/afat-nama`
   → *khilafat-nama*, `'a/im` → *ʿalim*, `ga/b` → *qalb*, `ha/` → *hal*, `Rih/a` → *Rihla*, `/hya'` →
   *Ihyaʾ*, `/slamic` → *Islamic*, `/s/amic` (**twice in one word**), `Gu/shan-i Ibrahimi`, `Ak/ibaru'l-
   Akhyar`, and the running headers `Si/silas` (p. 235, folio 215) and `Si/vilas` (p. 243, folio 223).
3. **`II` read as `11`, `Il`, `ll`, `1`, `U1`, `UW`, `\1` or `I11` — Roman volume and sura numerals are
   unreliable book-wide.** "A /iterary history of Persia, 11 / Ul / Il"; "Voyages D'Ibn Batoutah, IIl /
   Ill / 111"; "Ain, II]"; "A'in, IL" against "A'in, III" for the same reference on facing pages (pp. 271,
   272; folios 251, 252); "Qur'an, LXXIII, I-II"; "Qur'an, Cll, 1-8"; "Qur‘an, 1X, 34-5"; "Rigveda, |,
   p. 64" with a bare pipe for the volume. **No volume, sura or verse number in this book may enter an
   entry unchecked.**
4. **Digits destroyed inside dates and locators — the highest-risk class.** "he di@ on 16 Zulqa'da 825/1
   November 1422" (p. 272, folio 252); "died in 750/1349-S0" (p. 326, folio 304); "The Shaikh died in
   853/1449-S0" (p. 284, folio 264); "died on 26 Ramazan 842/12 March **14339**" (p. 372, folio 350) — **a
   five-digit CE year that cannot be entered in any form**; "781-1399" between "774/1372" and "785/1383"
   (p. 313, folio 291) — **impossible as printed**; "857-S8" in Hallaj's birth date (p. 73, folio 57);
   "(6. 6 Muharram 888/14 February 1483)" for *b. 6 Muharram* (p. 409, folio 385); "The fifth Guru, Arjan
   Dev (1563-81-—1606)" (p. 407, folio 383) — **unresolved, and three archive rows would take their date
   from it**; "1831-36/1913-18" (p. 447, folio 423) — **an AH/CE pair that cannot correspond**;
   "27 Ramazan 560,7 August 1165" (p. 121, folio 103) and "772 1370-71", "1332 1914", "1347 1968-69",
   "1313/1936-7" against "1313/1934-5" — **separators lost or wrong throughout.**
5. **Footnote markers and numerals corrupted on nearly every page of the book.** Digits read as `'`, `“`,
   `S`, `O`, `W`, `J`, `4`, `?`, `}`, `*`, `\`, `T`, `§`; sigla fused to their markers (`SAA`, `24A`,
   `\FF`, `IFF`, `ONU`, `TJamali`); note sequences that run 1, 2, 4 with no 3 (pp. 270, 275, 324, 344;
   folios 250, 255, 302, 322) and pages with two notes numbered 4 (pp. 172, 230, 270, 275). **Worst is
   p. 159 (folio 141), where ten notes print as `IFF 2FF 3FF 4K.A.Nizami SSA SFF 1FF 8FF 9SA WFF` and the
   footnote-to-text mapping cannot be reconstructed.** **No fact above is cited by footnote number where
   the number could not be matched to its callout; where a note is named, it is named by its content.**
6. **Lost word-spaces — the class that damages sentences rather than words.** "fellintoanecstaticswoon"
   (p. 216, folio 198); "inorder thatthey mighthave scholarly discussions" **inside the Ruknuʾd-Din
   three-things quotation** (p. 234, folio 214); "Theageiscalculated according tothelunar Hijri calendar"
   (p. 143, folio 125); "toteach him Persian" (p. 408, folio 384); "Namdevacame froma lowcaste family"
   (p. 378, folio 356); "picked upa seed" (p. 151, folio 133); "Al-Nasiralso builtanextensive khangah"
   (p. 104, folio 88); "Oneof the sonsof Muhammad,a son of" (p. 304, folio 282). **Every quotation in this
   file that sits inside such a run is flagged at the point of use and must be re-read against the print
   before it is quoted verbatim.**
7. **`II` → `11` and `l` → `1` inside numbers, plus `S`→`5`, `i`→`1`, `Z`→`7`, `}`→digit and `\`→digit in
   index and manuscript locators.** "f. Sa" for *f. 5a*; "f. S38b" for *538b*; "ff. 530a-b, S3la" for
   *531a*; "al-Budala, 4}"; "Kitab al-Ta’rifat, 39}"; "Tuhfat al-Nuzzar, \6"; "Satimaina Lor-Chandrani,
   37\"; "Tehran, 28, 31,43, 59, 7Z, 119"; "Voyages d'Ibn Batoutah, … 2123, …" — **a four-digit locator in
   a 467-folio book**; "Studies in Islamic mysticism, 58, 68, 71, 73-14" and "Mir'atu'l-Asrar, ff. 25b-25a"
   and "Mir'atul-Ayrar, ff. 482a-454a" — **three descending ranges, impossible as printed and not
   repaired.**
8. **Devanagari destroyed wholesale — the single largest defect in the book for this archive.** Every
   Devanagari footnote in Chapter Six is unreadable: the Hindawi verses of **Baba Farid**, Shaikh
   Hamiduʾd-Din Nagauri and Shaikh ʿAbduʾl-Quddus print only in Devanagari and the OCR of every one is
   garbage — "@ faert wm aa, fase (M1) aHrT!" (p. 349, folio 327), "1 gear wity cree afeareg qf qeet |"
   (p. 350, folio 328), and the same on pp. 358–365 (folios 336–343) and at pp. 387–388 (folios 365–366).
   **[illegible] for all of them. No original-script quotation can be taken from this book for
   `shrine-of-fariduddin-ganjshakar` or `gorakhnath-goraknath-temple`; only Rizvi's English renderings are
   usable, and for the Farid dohas those are translations of translations (Hindawi → Persian by Bilgarami
   → English by Rizvi, p. 350, folio 328). A re-OCR of the footnote band on pp. 349–365 with a Devanagari
   model would be worth commissioning.** Footnote 4 on p. 359 (folio 337) is lost entirely at the page
   break — **[illegible]** — and with it the support for the Onkar/sunyata equation.
9. **Two further passages destroyed beyond reading.** Footnote 5 on p. 216 (folio 198) reads in full "Pf
   47. lamaln op t$4-S8. AM. p. 276" — **[illegible]; siglum, work and all three page numbers
   unrecoverable, and it is the note supporting the Shaikh Ahmad Naharwani story.** The plate leaf p. 222
   (folio not stated) is roughly twenty lines of `[illegible]` OCR of the image itself; **only the caption
   survives** — "'Iraqi with the Qalandars from the Majalisu'l-'Ushshaq / Bodleian, dated 959/1552, Ms.
   Ouseley Add. 24, f. 79 R." — and p. 221 (folio not stated) has nothing but "igitized b Gor gle".
10. **Dropped drop-caps at section openings, cosmetic but disfiguring.** "USLIM" for *MUSLIM* (p. 12);
    "HE terms" for *THE terms* (p. 15); "Qu represents the inward or esoteric side of Islam" for *SUFISM*
    (p. 34, folio not stated) — **the book's definitional sentence**; "T#e" (p. 132, folio 114); "Qvrsm.
    as we have seen, is a very complex phenomenon" for *SUFISM* — **the first word of the Conclusion**
    (p. 421, folio not stated); "| Chapter One we discussed" for *IN* (p. 425); "HE founders" for *THE*
    (p. 428); "EFORE the advent of Islam" for *BEFORE* (p. 433).
11. **Small-capital `A.D.` read as lower-case letters** — "to 1600 ap" (p. 3), "By 1000 ab" (p. 48,
    folio 32), "the eighth to the end of the tenth centuries aD" (p. 49, folio 33), "1494-1538 ab"
    (p. 374, folio 352), "(1206-1556 av)" (p. 450, folio 426). **Harmless once recognised, but the era
    marker on a date can look like part of a word.**
12. **Google-Books scan furniture on every page** — "Google", "» Google", "+ Google", "Gor gle", "Google
    sie", "Digitized by Google UNIVERSITY OF CALIFORNIA", "CALIFORNIZ". **None of it is text and none is
    cited above.** It sits between the columns in the index, which is why recto folio numbers appear out of
    order there, and it has demonstrably disturbed the reading order at p. 466 (folio 442), where the
    orphaned fragment "Mufti" belongs to the "Ghulam Sarwar of Lahore" entry higher up the column. **In one
    place it splits an entry: "Zainu'l-‘Abidin, Sultan (1420-70), 290," ends column 1 of p. 491 and its
    remaining locator "350" resumes after the stamp (p. 491, folio 467).**
13. **The book's OWN transliteration conventions are not damage and must never be "corrected"** — it
    writes *s* for Arabic *th* and *z* for *dh* throughout ("Hadis", "ʿUsman", "Misaq", "zikr", "Zuʾn-Nun",
    "Tirmiz", "muhdas", "Haris"), "Saiyid" not *Sayyid*, "Shaikh" not *Shaykh*, "sama'" not *samāʿ*,
    "masnawi" not *mathnawi*, "sufi" lower-case, "Panjab" not *Punjab*, "Ajodhan" not *Pakpattan*,
    "Saiyidpur" not *Saidpur*, "Amanabad" not *Eminabad*, "Khattri" not *Khatri*, "Munyari" (defended in a
    footnote against *Maner*/*Muner*, p. 248, folio 228), "Nagauri", "Bada'un". **A worker who "fixes"
    these is altering the book.**

### 3. No index or endnote locator has become a `(p. N)` — and which rows rest on index evidence only

**RULE 6 is the governing rule for the last fifty pages of this book.** Chunks 026, 027 and 028 are
bibliography and index (**pp. 442–491, folios 418–467**), and chunk 021's footnote 6 (p. 358, folio 336)
and several of the book's own cross-references are printed-page pointers, not PDF pages. **Every number
string quoted in this file from those pages is quoted as the book prints it and is cited to the PDF page
the entry is printed on. Not one index or endnote locator has been converted into a `(p. N)` anywhere.**

**Rows whose evidence in this file is index- or bibliography-only, in whole or in decisive part, and which
a human must treat as pointers rather than as facts:**

- **`mazar-e-iqbal`** — two bibliography lines (p. 452, folio 428) and nothing else; the index's "Iqbal,
  163, 213" (p. 469, folio 445) is **not** mapped.
- **`gurdwara-rori-sahib`** (the Bhai Mardana half) — "Bhai Mardana, 384-85" (p. 462, folio 438), three
  words and two folios "I cannot follow".
- **`dargah-pir-ratan-nath-jee`** — one clause of main text (p. 354, folio 332), one editorial footnote
  (p. 376, folio 354) and one index headword (p. 482, folio 458). **The weakest mapping in this file.**
- **`sakhi-sarwar`, `shergarh`, `mazar-of-bulleh-shah` (as a person), `langer-makhdoom`,
  `gurdwara-dash-mesh-pita` and the five Guru Hargobind rows and `gurdwara-guru-ram-das-ji`** — the whole
  of their content is a **negative** finding in the index (pp. 462–483, folios 438–459).
- **`shrine-of-shah-rukn-e-alam`** — mapped on two index headwords as well as on the body text, and the
  two headwords are not linked by the index (p. 482, folio 458).
- **`gorakhnath-goraknath-temple` and `tilla-jogian`** — share one undifferentiated index entry (p. 466,
  folio 442), and two bibliography entries about him (pp. 449, 450; folios 425, 426).
- **The eighteen Guru Nanak rows** — their index support is one headword and seven monographs (p. 466,
  folio 442; pp. 451–455, folios 427–431); **the index names no gurdwara at all.**
- **`shrine-of-miran-hussain-zanjani-zanjani-sahib`** — the index headword's nisba is corrupt and the
  Chah Miran headword's number string is destroyed (pp. 468, 463; folios 444, 439).

**The index is also demonstrably not exhaustive**, so an absent folio in an entry is not evidence that the
book is silent there: "Shaikh Jalal Thaneswari, 348, 399" omits folio 365 where the book treats him at
length; "Shiva, 331-34, 337, 349-50, 355, 365, 368" omits folio 367, where the Shiva–Shakti passage of
Qutban stands; "Sana’i … 18, 79, 81, 209, 362" omits 363 (pp. 484–486, folios 460–462).

### 4. Dates that disagree, row by row

| row | the book prints | the archive row | status |
|---|---|---|---|
| `data-darbar` | three positions on one page — tomb tablet 465/1072-73; Nicholson, between 465 and 469/1076-77; **Rizvi's own: after 1089** (p. 130, folio 112); index "d. after 1089" (p. 468, folio 444) | `figure_died` **1072** | **The row prints the tomb-tablet reading, which is the one Rizvi explicitly argues against. A human must decide whether the entry keeps 1072, prints all three, or follows the book.** |
| `shrine-of-bahauddin-zakariya` | **7 Safar, 661/21 December, 1262** (p. 212, folio 194) and "died in December 1262" (p. 226, folio 206); index "d. 1262" (p. 461, folio 437) | `figure_died` **1267** | **A five-year gap, too large for a calendar-conversion rounding, and the printed forms carry no OCR damage. Must be checked before either date enters an entry.** |
| `shrine-of-fariduddin-ganjshakar` | **5 Muharram 664/17 October 1265** (pp. 158, 174; folios 140, 156), "death in 1265" (p. 330, folio 308); index "d. 1265" (p. 461, folio 437) | `figure_died` **1266** | **A one-year discrepancy, consistent across four statements in the book. Not reconciled.** The book's main text never states the death; it is carried only in a footnote whose own purpose is to attack another chronology (p. 174, folio 156, footnote 9), and an entry should cite it as "AA, p. 54, as reported by Rizvi". |
| `shrine-of-fariduddin-ganjshakar` (birth) | **569/1173-74 or 571/1175-76** (p. 157, folio 139); index "b. 1173-74 or 1175-76" | no birth year | **The book supplies a date the archive lacks, and prefers neither of its two.** |
| `shrine-of-shah-rukn-e-alam` | **735/1334-35** (p. 234, folio 214); index "(d. 1334—35)" (p. 482, folio 458) | `figure_died` **1335** | **Apparent only: 735 AH is 1334-35, and neither figure has been altered.** |
| `shrine-of-miran-hussain-zanjani-zanjani-sahib` | death dated only by Hujwiri's arrival, **"c. 1035"**, itself hedged (p. 130, folio 112) | `figure_died` **1042** | **The row's date is not in the book at all.** |
| `shrine-of-makhdoom-jahaniyan-jahangasht` | **b. 15 Shaʿban 707/9 February 1308, d. 10 Zuʾlhijja 785/3 February 1384** (pp. 299, 303; folios 277, 281); index "(b. 1308, d. 1384)" | `figure_died` **1384** | **Agreement, and the only archive date this book confirms.** |
| `tomb-of-qutbuddin-aibak` | **"(1206-1210)"**, a reign span (p. 481, folio 457) | `figure_died` **1210** | **The archive's year is the span's terminal year; the book gives no death date.** |
| `gurdwara-darbar-sahib-kartarpur` / the Nanak rows | **b. 1469, d. 1539** (pp. 407, 418; folios 383, 394; index p. 466, folio 442) | `figure_died` **1539** | **Agreement.** |
| `gurdwara-dera-sahib` (Guru Arjan) | **"(1563-81-—1606)"** (p. 407, folio 383) and index "(b. 1563 guru 1581-1606)" (p. 466, folio 442) | `figure_died` **1606** | **The body-text string is damaged and has NOT been resolved; the index's terminal year matches, which is a reason to check the string, not a reason to read it that way.** |
| `lal-shahbaz-qalandar` | **no death date anywhere**, and the name is "Mir Saiyid ʿUsman", not "Usman Marwandi" (p. 328, folio 306) | `figure_died` **1274**, figure "Usman Marwandi" | **Neither the year nor the nisba may be taken from this book.** |
| `shrine-of-jalaluddin-surkh-posh-bukhari-jalaluddin-bukhari` | **no date of any kind** (p. 223, folio 203) | `figure_died` **1291** | **Neither confirmed nor contradicted.** |

### 5. Name collisions — the book's standing traps, and what was and was not mapped

- **"Qutbuʾd-Din" is at least NINE different men in this book, and the bare short form is never mapped.**
  (i) **Sultan Qutbuʾd-Din Aibak** (pp. 139, 140, 153; folios 121, 122, 135) — **the only one mapped, to
  `tomb-of-qutbuddin-aibak`**; (ii) Khwaja Qutbuʾd-Din Bakhtiyar Kaki, the Delhi Chishti (pp. 143, 152–156,
  166, 170, 211, 214, 239, 240, 246, 298, 326–327, 348; folios 125, 134–138, 148, 152, 193, 196, 219, 220,
  226, not stated, 304–305, 326); (iii) the Qutb Minar, a building (p. 155, folio 137; index p. 481,
  folio 457); (iv) Shaikh Qutbuʾd-Din Dabir (p. 200, folio 182); (v) Maulana Qutbuʾd-Din Kashani (pp. 209,
  216; folios 191, 198); (vi) Sultan Qutbuʾd-Din Mubarak Shah Khalji (pp. 213, 242; folios 195, 222);
  (vii) Shaikh Qutbuʾd-Din, later Chisht Khan, a fifteenth-century Mandu courtier (p. 295, folio 275);
  (viii) Sultan Qutbuʾd-Din of Kashmir, r. 1373–89 (pp. 313, 314, 318; folios 291, 292, 296); (ix) Sultan
  Qutbuʾd-Din Ahmad of Gujarat, 1451–59 (p. 431, folio 407). **Add Shaikh Nur Qutb-i ʿAlam and Qutb-i ʿAlam
  of Pandua, who share only the element *Qutb*.** **A "Shaikh Qutbu'd-Din" with a tomb at Delhi (p. 276,
  folio 256) is emphatically not the Anarkali memorial.**
- **"Ruknuʾd-Din" is at least eight men, and the index prints six of them in one column.** "Ruknu'd-Din
  Abu'l-Fath, 282", "Ruknu'd-Din Ibrahim, 303", "Ruknu'd-Din, Khwaja, 228", "Ruknu'd-Din Muhammad, 118",
  "Ruknu'd-Din Multani, Shaikh (d. 1334—35), …", "Ruknu'd-Din Samargandi, Maulana, 197", "Ruknu'd-Din,
  Shaikh, 7, 342-43, 345-46, 365", "Ruknu'd-Din b. Shihabu'd-Din, 241" (p. 482, folio 458). **Only the
  first and the fifth are mapped to `shrine-of-shah-rukn-e-alam`, on the *kunya* and on the death range
  respectively; they may be one man under two headwords or two men, and the index does not say.** Outside
  the index the book's other Ruknuʾd-Dins are: Maulana Ruknuʾd-Din Samarqandi, an opponent of samaʿ
  (p. 215, folio 197); Ruknuʾd-Din Ibrahim, a son of Iltutmish (p. 223, folio 203); Khwaja Ruknuʾd-Din of
  Kilukhari, a Firdawsi khalifa (p. 248, folio 228); **"the Chishti, Shaikh Ruknu'd-Din, son of Shaikh
  Shihabu'd-Din" of Delhi (p. 261, folio 241)**; **Saiyid Ruknuʾd-Din Abuʾl-Fath, son of Saiyid Hamid
  Kabir, of Uch, four generations after Makhdum-i Jahaniyan (p. 304, folio 282)** — **this last is the
  most dangerous of all, because it reproduces the archive row's full name at the wrong place and the
  wrong century**; Shaikh Ruknuʾd-Din ʿAlaʾuʾd-Dawla Simnani, a Kubrawi (p. 342, folio 320); Qazi
  Ruknuʾd-Din Abu Hamid Muhammad bin Muhammad al-Amidi of Samarqand (p. 357, folio 335); and the son of
  ʿAbduʾl-Quddus Gangohi, b. 5 Jumada I 897/5 March 1492, Chishti-Sabiri, who "died in AD 1575-76"
  (pp. 364–365, 368, 387; folios 342–343, 346, 365). **None of those is mapped.** **Chunk 011's
  "Shaikhu'l-Islam Shaikh Ruknu'd-Din Multani led Shaikh Nizamu'd-Din's funeral prayer" (p. 181, folio 163)
  was left unmapped by its worker and has not been mapped here either, although the title, the nisba, the
  city and the date (1325) all fit; a human who confirms it gives this row a dated, named act.**
- **"Farid" is at least four men, and the book never disambiguates them.** (i) **Baba Farid Ganj-i Shakar
  of Ajodhan — the only one mapped**; (ii) **Shaikh Faridu'd-Din Mahmud of Nagaur**, grandson and successor
  of Shaikh Hamiduʾd-Din, whose sons and disciples (Nakhshabi, Shaikh Kabir, Khwaja Husain Nagauri) are on
  pp. 148–149 (folios 130–131) and **must not migrate onto the Pakpattan row**; (iii) **Shaikh Faridu'd-Din
  ʿAttar**, the poet of Nishapur, "born in 537/1142-43… died there in 617/1220" (pp. 96, 150, 217–218, 256;
  folios 80, 132, 199–200, 236); (iv) **Maulana Faridu'd-Din**, a Firdawsi who compiled the *Fatawa-i Tatar
  Khaniyya* in 777/1375—76 (p. 248, folio 228). **A fifth "Farid" is Sher Shah Suri — "the concern of Farid
  over the tyranny of village officials. He later became Sher Shah" (p. 406, folio 382, footnote 5).** The
  mapping of the Ajodhan Farid rests on the book's *form of name*: it writes "Baba Farid" and "the Baba"
  for him and not for the others. **That is a form-of-name argument, not evidence, and three of this row's
  bullets rest on it alone: the plate caption (p. 298, folio not stated), the Delhi-cell sentence (p. 276,
  folio 256) and the patronymic at p. 397 (folio 373). A human should confirm all three.** Also `Shaikh
  Kabir b. Shaikh Farid, 131` (p. 485, folio 461) is **withheld**: the index does not say which Farid.
- **"Jalaluʾd-Din" is at least six men, and two archive rows collide inside the name.** The book itself
  separates **Shaikh Jalaluʾd-Din Tabrizi** (Baghdad → Multan → Delhi → Badaʾun → Bengal, pp. 217–220,
  237–238, 246, 277, 280, 337; folios 199–202, 217–218, 226, 257, 260, 315) from **Saiyid Jalaluʾd-Din
  Surkh (Red) Bukhari** (Bukhara → Bhakkar → Uch, p. 223, folio 203) and from **Saiyid Jalaluʾd-Din
  Bukhari, the Makhdum-i Jahaniyan, the Jahangasht** (b. 1308, d. 1384, buried Uch, pp. 299–303, folios
  277–281). **Tabrizi is mapped to nothing, in every chunk, and the evidence points away from both Uch rows
  rather than between them.** The remaining three: Shaikh Jamaluʾd-Din of Sawa (a different name element),
  the last Khwarazm-Shah Jalaluʾd-Din (p. 84, folio 68), Shaikh Jalal Thaneswari d. 1581-82 (pp. 368, 387;
  folios 346, 365), Shaikh Jalal of Sylhet (pp. 314–316, folios 292–294) and Sultan Jalaluʾd-Din Muhammad
  Shah of Bengal (p. 280, folio 260). **The index's cross-reference "Jalalu’d-Din Bukhari, see Makhdum
  Jahaniyan" (p. 470, folio 446) would send a worker looking for the Surkh-Posh row to the Jahaniyan row's
  folios. Do not follow it: a consolidator matching on the string "Jalaluddin Bukhari" will get the two
  rows backwards.** The nisba "Bukhari" alone is shared by **five** archive rows and can never carry a
  mapping by itself.
- **"Burhanuʾd-Din" is at least eight men** (index p. 463, folio 439, plus p. 481, folio 457, plus
  Burhanuʾd-Din Gharib at p. 201, folio 183, and Shaikh Burhan of Kalpi at p. 394, folio 370, and Makhdum
  Shaikh Budhan at p. 389, folio 367); **none is `langer-makhdoom`'s Makhdoom Burhan-ud-din of Chiniot.**
- **"Badruʾd-Din" is three men and one story conflates two of them** — Shaikh Badruʾd-Din of Ghazna,
  d. "about 657/1258-59", buried at Delhi (p. 156, folio 138); Badruʾd-Din Ghaznawi (p. 158, folio 140),
  **probably the same man, but the book does not say so**; and Maulana Badruʾd-Din Ishaq, steward of the
  Ajodhan jamaʿat-khana (p. 160, folio 142). **In the qalandar episode the disciple is called "Shaikh
  Badru'd-Din", then "Shaikh Badru'd-Din Ishaq", then "Badru'd-Din" within eight lines (p. 160, folio 142)
  — from the setting he must be the steward, but the text gives him the Ghazna Shaikh's title.** Add
  Khwaja Badruʾd-Din Samarqandi, the Firdawsi founder (pp. 246–247, folios 226–227).
- **"Bahaʾuʾd-Din" is at least five men** — Bahaʾuʾd-Din Walad, Rumi's father (p. 97, folio 81); Khwaja
  Bahaʾuʾd-Din Naqshband (p. 287, folio 267); the Jaunpuri disciple of Shaikh Muhammad ʿIsa (p. 283,
  folio 263); Saiyid Bahaʾuʾd-Din, son of Jalaluʾd-Din Surkh (p. 299, folio 277) — **NOT
  `tomb-of-baha-al-halim-uch-sharif`, whose figure is "Hazrat Baha'al-Halim"** — and **Shaikh
  Bahaʾuʾd-Din Zakariyya**, the only one mapped. **`Shaikh Baha‘u’d-Din, 263` (p. 485, folio 461) is
  withheld: the index gives it no nisba, no date and no Multan, there is no `Zakariyya` headword anywhere
  in the Z-run, and folio 263 sits in a cluster of fifteenth-century men.**
- **Further collisions considered and refused, recorded so nobody re-opens them.** `peer-makki` against
  "Shaikh Rashid Makki" (p. 140, folio 122) and "Saiyid Muhammad Husaini bin Ja'far al-Makki" (p. 264,
  folio 244); `shah-jamal` and `shrine-of-hafiz-muhammad-jamal-multani` against "Shaikh Jamal … at Kol
  (Aligarh)" (p. 139, folio 121), "Shaikh Jamal, a Muslim Gujar" of Awadh (p. 292, folio 272), the
  historian "Jamali" / "Dervish Jamali Kanbo Dihlawi" d. 1536 (pp. 308–309, folios 286–287) and "Saiyid
  Jamalu'd-Din Bukhari" of Kashmir (p. 321, folio 299) — **the last agrees with `shah-jamal` on Jamal,
  Bukhari and Suhrawardi and disagrees on place and on every date, and is flagged, not mapped**;
  `shrine-of-shah-shams-ud-din-sabzwari` against "Shamsu'd-Din Muhammad Tabrizi" (p. 98, folio 82), "Shaikh
  Shamsu'd-Din Turk … from Panipat" (p. 290, folio 270) and "Mir Shamsu'd-Din 'Iraqi" of 1481 (pp. 320–321,
  folios 298–299); `shrine-of-pir-baba-syed-ali-tirmizi` against the bare "Tirmizi" (p. 115, folio 97),
  "Maulana Minhaju'd-Din Tirmizi" (p. 157, folio 139) and the index's "Tirmizi, see al-Hakim al-Tirmizi"
  (p. 489, folio 465) — **the surname is a known trap**; `shrine-of-baba-shah-chiragh` against "Shaikh
  Nasiru'd-Din Chiragh Dihlawi" (pp. 234, 238; folios 214, 218); `shrine-of-baba-shah-kamal` against
  "Shaikh Kamal alias Shaikh Kalu" of Kara (p. 284, folio 264) and `Shaikh Kamal, 264-65` (p. 485,
  folio 461); `darbar-ghamkol-sharif-zinda-pir` against "Zinda Ghazi" of the Bengali Panch Pir (p. 339,
  folio 317) and `Shaikh Ahmad Jam Zinda Pil (d. 1141), 137` (p. 484, folio 460); `khawaja-feroz-ud-din-
  gharib-nawaz` against Khwaja Muʿinuʾd-Din Chishti, **who has no archive row at all and whose entire Ajmer
  body of material would land on the wrong row if a consolidator worked from the epithet "Gharib Nawaz"
  rather than from the printed page — the book never prints that epithet**; `sevapanthi-darbar-bhai-gurdas-
  gandava` and `khatwari-darbar-shikarpur` against "Bhai Gurdas Bhalla" (pp. 384, 408; folios 362, 384) —
  **a three-way ambiguity, since the archive's two Bhai Gurdas rows are themselves two different men**;
  `darbar-malik-ahmad-ayaz` against the book's abundant Mahmud of Ghazna material (pp. 83, 136, 311; folios
  67, 118, 289) — **Ayaz himself is never named**; `bari-imam` against "Bari Dargah, 317" (p. 462,
  folio 438); `gurdwara-babay-nanki` against "Guru Nanak's brother-in-law, Jai Ram" (p. 409, folio 385) —
  **Bebe Nanaki is never named**; `purana-bhalwal` and `darbar-hazrat-khawaja-shah-muhammad-sulaiman-
  taunsvi-r-a` against "Shaikh Sulaiman" (p. 357, folio 335) and `Srimat Sulaiman, 371` (p. 487,
  folio 463); `tahir-bandagi-qadri` and `shrine-at-odero-lal-udero-lal-teerath-asthan` against "Baba Tahir"
  of Hamadan–Luristan (p. 92, folio 76), "Tahir bin al-Husain" (p. 82, folio 66) and `Tahirid dynasty, 66`
  (p. 488, folio 464); `tomb-of-ustad-nuriya` against "Nuri, 61" and "Nuriyya, 92" (p. 479, folio 455);
  `gurdwara-singh-sabha` and `gurdwara-shaheed-ganj-singh-singhnian` against `Sikhs, 383-84, 388, 394-95`
  (p. 486, folio 462); and `shrine-of-hazrat-shah-daula-daryai` against "Gujarat" and "Gujarati Sultanate"
  (p. 466, folio 442) — **the western Indian province, not Gujrat.**
- **Other men the book itself does not separate, which a consolidator must keep apart**: three "Abu Saʿid"s
  within five pages (pp. 138, 142; folios 120, 124); two Hasans (pp. 42–45, folios 26–29); two
  Husamuʾd-Dins (pp. 284–285, 295; folios 264–265, 275); two Abuʾl-Faths (pp. 282–283, 294; folios 262–263,
  274); two Sadruʾd-Dins, one of Multan and one of Quniya, **whom p. 226 (folio 206) and p. 225 (folio 205)
  give incompatible roles** ; "Shaikh Nasiru'd-Din Mahmud" and "Shaikh Nasiru'd-Din Chiragh-i Dihli", given
  as the same man without the book saying so (pp. 281, 294; folios 261, 274); "Simnani" for two men in one
  paragraph (pp. 286–287, folios 266–267); two Bibi Fatimas with four name-forms (pp. 426–427, folios
  402–403); and "Hamidu'd-Din", who may be one man or two (pp. 135, 141, 145; folios 117, 123, 127).

### 6. Placement doubts — the mappings a human should check first

1. **`dargah-pir-ratan-nath-jee` is the weakest mapping in this file.** A bare name in a list (p. 354,
   folio 332), a bare name in a corrective footnote (p. 376, folio 354) and a bare index headword (p. 482,
   folio 458). **The book writes "Ratannath"; the archive writes "Baba Pir Ratan Nath". The identification
   rests on an unusual name plus Nath affiliation plus the chapter's placing of the Nath centre at
   Peshawar, which is the row's town — and a human should confirm that the archive's Ratan Nath is the
   Nath Siddha and not a later namesake.** **Separately and more seriously: the book's "Shaikh Baba Ratan
   or Hajji Baba Ratan" (p. 342, folio 320) — "Born before the birth of Muhammad", made a companion of the
   Prophet, given "Abu'l-Riza" as his kunniya, Tabarhinda or Bhatinda in the Panjab as his origin and
   700/1300 as his death, and buried in the Punjab — is left UNMAPPED, and Rizvi's own footnote reads as
   distinguishing him from Ratannath (p. 376, folio 354, footnote 1). The two figures are conflated in
   some traditions; if they are one man the archive row's category ("Hindu Temple") would have to be
   reconsidered, so this is a row-level question and not merely a naming one.**
2. **`shrine-of-shah-rukn-e-alam` was three earlier workers' weakest mapping and one worker found positive
   reason against it.** What settles it: the full name "Shaikh Ruknu'd-Din Abu'l Fath" (p. 230, folio 210),
   matching the row's own parenthetical name; the descent from Sadruʾd-Din ʿArif and Bahaʾuʾd-Din Zakariyya
   (pp. 230–231, folios 210–211); Multan as his seat (pp. 233, 235, 299; folios 213, 215, 277); and the
   death year 735/1334-35 (p. 234, folio 214). **What does NOT settle it: the book never prints
   "Rukn-e-Alam", never gives him a tomb, and prints a second man of almost exactly the row's full name —
   "Saiyid Ruknu'd-Din Abu'l-Fath, the son of Saiyid Hamid Kabir" of Uch (p. 304, folio 282). A
   consolidator reading p. 304 alone would map the wrong man.**
3. **`data-darbar`'s title-only bullet stretches the 18 September ruling one step.** At p. 189 (folio 171)
   the book prints only "Kashfu'l-Mahjub" inside Amir Khusraw's list of styles; **the person is not named
   at all**, and the link to the row's principal figure rests on the authorship the book states elsewhere
   (p. 109, folio 91). **A consolidator who will not accept a title-only mention should delete that
   bullet; nothing else depends on it.** The same judgement covers the single subordinate clause at p. 324
   (folio 302), which carries no fact about the man, the book or the shrine.
4. **`shrine-of-bahauddin-zakariya` has four single-clause bullets** — p. 153 (folio 135), p. 292
   (folio 272), p. 299 (folio 277), p. 413 (folio 389) — **each a bare name with no date, no place and no
   act, and none of them shrine evidence.**
5. **`shrine-of-makhdoom-jahaniyan-jahangasht` at p. 295 (folio 275) and p. 429 (folio 405) is mapped by
   transfer.** The book there prints only "Saiyid Jalal Bukhari" and "the Makhdum Jahaniyan" with no
   personal name; the transfer rests on the equation "Makhdum Jahaniyan Saiyid Jalal Bukhari" at p. 292
   (folio 272). **If a reviewer rejects the transfer, drop those two bullets and keep the rest.**
   **Sharper: at p. 342 (folio 320) the book prints the compound "Makhdum Jahaniyan Saiyid Jalal Bukhari",
   which fuses the grandson's title with the name that is the handle for the grandfather's row. The
   material there is mapped by the title — but if the Jalali order is in fact traced to the grandfather,
   the Shiʿism, the nakedness, the fire-sitting, the cap and spiritual tree and the snakes move wholesale
   to `shrine-of-jalaluddin-surkh-posh-bukhari-jalaluddin-bukhari`. This is the mapping most worth a
   human's eye.**
6. **`gorakhnath-goraknath-temple`'s site mapping is the file's single riskiest and also its single best
   shared-ground find.** The book never uses the word "temple" of Gorakhtari, never names a deity there,
   never names Gorakhnath in that passage, and preserves Trumpp's doubt that the place exists at all
   (p. 413, folio 389). **The mapping rests on the site name plus the city plus McLeod's identification.
   If a reviewer judges that insufficient, the site material moves to an unmapped flag and the row keeps
   only the figure evidence.**
7. **`tilla-jogian` receives the whole Gorakhnath block on the many-row-person rule and on nothing else.**
   **The book never names Tilla Jogian, the Salt Range, Jhelum or Dina, and "Tilla Jogian" is not an index
   headword.** The Peshawar site material is **not** this row's.
8. **Fifteen of the eighteen Guru Nanak rows rest on the person and on no site whatever.** Only
   `gurudwara-janam-asthan-nankana-sahib` (Talwandi/Nankana Sahib as birthplace), `gurdwara-darbar-sahib-
   kartarpur` (death at Kartarpur in 1539) and `gurdwara-rori-sahib` (through Bhai Mardana, its second
   principal figure) have any site-specific support. **The biography of pp. 407–419 (folios 383–395) must
   not be written into fifteen site entries as though the book described those sites; it describes none of
   them, and the book states that Guru Nanak left no material memorial at all (p. 413, folio 389).**
9. **`tomb-of-qutbuddin-aibak` rests on a bare-name hit and a footnote about a slave purchase.** The row
   is a Lahore memorial; the book places Aibak at Delhi and Ghazni only and gives him no tomb, no death
   date and no Lahore.
10. **`shrine-of-miran-hussain-zanjani-zanjani-sahib` is mapped on the burial place, which is an exact
    match to the row's location, against a name that differs by two honorifics the book never prints.**
    **A second Husain Zinjani cannot be ruled out from the book alone, but the coincidence of Khattali's
    discipleship, the Panjab posting, the Lahore death and Chah Miran makes one man overwhelmingly likely.
    Flagged rather than asserted.**
11. **`mazar-e-iqbal` rests on two English philosophy titles.** The row's figure is a poet; **the book
    prints not one line of his verse and no Urdu or Persian title of his.**
12. **`bhit-bhit-shah` is NOT mapped, and it is the closest call in the bibliography.** "Shah 'Abdu'l-
    Latif, Silsila-i 'Aliya-i Chishtiyya-i Nizamiya-i Fakhriyya-i Sulaimaniyya-i Latifiyya, Tippera, 1917."
    (p. 447, folio 423). **Three things in the printed entry argue against the Sindhi poet and none is
    general knowledge: the imprint is Tippera 1917, the work is a Chishti-Nizami-Fakhri-Sulaimani chain,
    and the chain's last element "Latifiyya" makes the author its eponym. The book gives no dates, no place
    of origin and no other work, so the identification cannot be closed either way. If it reverses, the id
    is `bhit-bhit-shah` and the evidence is one bibliography line.**

### 7. The hedge conversion: what was converted, and the count

**Thirty-five hedges have been converted and their hedge language deleted.** Every one named its rows
conditionally in the Pass 1 Doubts ("under interim convention (a) it takes no id", "if the toponym ruling
reverses, the rows are …", "unmapped for a multi-row collision", "if deity-names count as principal
figures, this bullet takes …"); **none of them decided anything new, and each is now a real mapping in the
archive section above.** The breakdown:

- **Twenty-five toponym hedges, under ruling (a) of 21 September 2026**, from chunks 001 (three: the
  Gorakhattri caption, Qasur, and the Lahore/Multan mentions of pp. 1–29), 007 (three: the Uch foundation
  legend, the five Multan mentions, the bare Lahore settings), 008 (two: Sabzwar, and the three Lahore
  mentions), 009 (one, covering Multan, Lahore, Uch and Qasur), 010 (one, covering Sehwan, Multan and Uch),
  011 (Multan), 012 (Multan), 013 (Multan and Uch), 014 (Lahore), 015 (Uch and Multan), 017 (Multan), 018
  (Sehwan), 020 (Multan, Uch and Lahore), 021 (Peshawar), 022 (Multan and Lahore), 023 (the "Babur visiting
  Gorkhattri" plate caption), 024 (Sialkot and Lahore), 025 (Uch and Multan), 027 (the index's bare
  toponyms) and 028 (Sehwan and Uch).
- **Ten person- and deity-level hedges, under the many-row-person rule of 22 September 2026**: the
  Gorakhnath two-row collision that chunks 021, 023, 026 and 027 all left open (**now both rows**); the Guru
  Nanak withholding in chunks 021, 023 and 026 (**now all eighteen rows**); the Guru Arjan withholding in
  chunk 021 (**now all three rows**); and the deity withholdings for **Shiva** (eight rows), **Krishna**
  (two), **Rama**, **Lakshmi-Narayan**, **Jagannath**, **Varuna** and **the Jain Tirthankaras** (three).

**Two corrections made while converting.** (i) Chunk 027's note says Karachi would take "the eight Karachi
rows"; the ruled join gives **eleven**, and the ruled list has been used. (ii) Several notes speak of
"the eighteen-odd Lahore rows" or "over thirty of them"; the ruled count is **35**, and the 35 ids are
printed in full on the Lahore line.

**Three conversions are incomplete for want of a page reference and a human must finish them.** Chunk 001's
four Lahore and two Multan mentions (pp. 1–29) and chunk 027's "Karachi", "Lahore", "Peshawar", "Nankana
Sahib" and "Kartarpur" index headwords (pp. 463–483) are named in the Pass 1 Doubts **without their printed
pages**, so those mappings are recorded at chunk level and cited to the chunk's page range. **No page has
been invented.**

**Two conversions are flagged as reaching beyond the letter of the ruling, and a reviewer may undo either
without touching anything else.** (i) **Sabzwar → `shrine-of-shah-shams-ud-din-sabzwari`**: ruling (a)
joins on `location_short`, and Sabzwar is a town in Iran that matches no archive location — the link is the
row's figure's *nisba*, and the Pass 1 worker named the row on that basis (pp. 138–139, folios 120–121).
**The material — "Sabzwar was notorious for Shi‘i sympathies", "a hotbed of schism, its inhabitants being
all fanatics", and the Badaʾuni anecdote about the search for a man named Abu Bakr — bears on how that
row's saint is read, and the book does not connect the two.** (ii) **The deity conversions beyond Shiva,
Krishna and Gorakhnath** — Rama, Lakshmi-Narayan, Jagannath, Varuna and the Tirthankaras — extend by the
same principle the ruling names for those three, but the ruling enumerates only those three, and in every
case the book names a deity inside a mystic's vocabulary and never a temple, a site or an act of worship
at a place.

### 8. Hedges NOT converted, because they are reasoned refusals and not conventions

**These are decisions the Pass 1 workers took on the evidence, and ruling (a) does not touch them.**

- **`sharada-peeth` — not mapped, and the reason is evidential.** The book has eight Kashmir entries and
  four pages of Kashmir narrative (pp. 311–314, 411, 442–455; folios 289–292, 387, 418–431), **and every
  one concerns Muslim Kashmir: Srinagar, Shihabuʾd-Din Pura, the Kali Mandir, the Rishis, Mir Saiyid ʿAli
  Hamadani. Sharda, the Neelum valley and Saraswati are named nowhere.** "Kashmir" is also not a key in the
  ruled join.
- **`bari-imam` — not mapped.** "Bari Dargah, 317" (p. 462, folio 438) sits at a folio the index otherwise
  gives to Bengal, Bihar, Arakan and Buddhist material, and Sayyid Abdul Latif Kazmi is named nowhere.
- **`dargah-pir-ratan-nath-jee` — "Baba Ratan Haijji" NOT mapped**, per Rizvi's own corrective footnote
  (p. 376, folio 354, footnote 1), and per the index's "Baba Ratan, Hajji, 320, 354" (p. 461, folio 437),
  **styled *Hajji*, listed at the same folio 320 as "Bhatinda", and carrying no Nath, no Peshawar and no
  Jhanda Bazar.**
- **The three Kali rows — `kalat-kali-temple`, `kali-bari-mandir`, `kalka-cave-temple-asthan-of-kalka-devi`
  — not mapped.** The book names "Kali Mandir in Srinagar", its Brahman priest, its demolition and the
  khanqah built on its site (pp. 314, 318; folios 292, 296). **All three archive Kali rows are elsewhere —
  Peshawar, Kalat and Aror — so even on a deity ruling the location is wrong. This is a
  deity-named-but-site-elsewhere instance, not a mapping.**
- **`gurdwara-sahib-saidpur-guru-nanak-dev-ji` and the three Eminabad rows — the "Saiyidpur (Amanabad)"
  episode NOT attached.** The book's own gloss equates "Saiyidpur" with "Amanabad" (p. 410, folio 386); the
  archive has `gurdwara-sahib-saidpur-guru-nanak-dev-ji` at Saidpur Village, Margalla Hills, Islamabad, and
  three rows at Eminabad, Gujranwala. **The captivity-and-massacre episode of 926/1520 — the one datable
  event in Guru Nanak's life in this book — has been recorded under `gurdwara-rori-sahib` only because Bhai
  Mardana is named in it, NOT because Amanabad has been placed. A human must establish which town the book
  means before it is attached to any of the four.** The index's "Amnabad, 385" (p. 462, folio 438) is
  **left as printed**: an m/i confusion would give "Aminabad", the folio 385 matches both "Bhai Mardana,
  384-85" and "Babur, Emperor (1526-30), 311, 345, 370, 385-89" (p. 461, folio 437), **but the reading has
  not been made and ruling (a) supplies the convention, not the identification.**
- **`gurudwara-janam-asthan-nankana-sahib` — "Talwandi, 383" (p. 489, folio 465) NOT equated with Nankana
  Sahib from the index.** The book does make that equation in its body text (p. 407, folio 383) and the row
  is mapped there; **the index headword by itself says nothing, and supplying the equation from it would be
  general knowledge.**
- **The Lahore imprints — NOT converted.** "published in Lahore in 1909", "Oriental College Magazine,
  Lahore X/1" (pp. 300, 310; folios 278, 288) and the bibliography's Lahore imprints are publication data,
  not places in the narrative.
- **`shri-varun-dev-mandir` and the deity question in general.** The ruling of 22 September names Shiva,
  Goraknath and Krishna as many-row principal figures, which settles that a deity can be a mapping handle;
  **it does not enumerate Varuna, Rama, Jagannath, Lakshmi-Narayan or the Tirthankaras, and those five
  conversions are extensions by the same principle. Rauf should confirm them.** Against them stands the
  Pass 1 judgement, recorded here because it is the honest reading: "in every case the book is naming a
  deity or a divine name inside a mystic's vocabulary, never a temple, a site or an act of worship at a
  place — which is an argument against the reversal".
- **`gori-temple-gori-jo-mandar` — the index's "Parasnath, 332" (p. 480, folio 456) is NOT the ground of
  the mapping.** On the page it stands in the index's run of Nath names at the same folio as Ratannath,
  where it reads as a Nath and not as a Tirthankara. **The mapping rests on the body text (p. 354,
  folio 332), where the ordinal is given: "Parasnath, probably Parsava, the twenty-third Jain
  tirthankara".**
- **`langer-makhdoom`, `shergarh`, `sakhi-sarwar` and Bulleh Shah as a person — absences, checked headword
  by headword, not failures to find.** See their subsections above.
- **`Shaktipeeth Shri Hinglaj Mata Mandir` — an ARCHIVE DEFECT, not a mapping question.** **That row still
  has an EMPTY id cell in `shrine_index.tsv`.** Hinglaj is not mentioned anywhere in the 28 chunks of this
  book, and the index breaks off at "Bukhara" (p. 462, folio 438) so the H run is unreachable — **the
  negative is partly a boundary effect, not a demonstration of absence from the book.** **Rauf must supply
  an id; none has been invented here, and every bold id in this file is copied verbatim from column 1 of
  `shrine_index.tsv`.**

### 9. Contradictions inside the book, kept side by side and not resolved

- **Shaikh Ruknuʾd-Din's visits to Delhi do not add up.** "visited Delhi twice" under ʿAlaʾuʾd-Din Khalji
  (p. 231, folio 211), then "His three visits to the capital during Sultan Qutbu'd-Din's reign" (p. 231,
  folio 211), then "A visit to Delhi by Shaikh Ruknu'd-Din, **described as his fourth**" under Ghiyasuʾd-Din
  Tughluq (p. 232, folio 212). **Two plus three is five, not three, and the fifth is called the fourth.
  The 700,000-tankas-per-visit figure must not be multiplied out until this is checked.**
- **Two incompatible accounts of Bahaʾuʾd-Din Zakariyya's estate, from two named sources on one page.**
  Jamali: Sadruʾd-Din alone inherited 700,000 tankas and gave it all away. "The Sururu's-Sudur, however,
  gives a different story": a cursed son abducted by robbers who took his share and then extracted
  Sadruʾd-Din's as well (p. 220, folio 202). **Rizvi declines to choose.**
- **Two incompatible successions after Shaikh Ruknuʾd-Din** — Jamali's nephew Shaikh Ismaʿil against Ibn
  Battuta's grandson Shaikh Hud (p. 234, folio 214).
- **Two incompatible accounts of the Multan intercession** — ʿIsami's seven days of meditation against
  Barani's immediate approach (p. 233, folio 213), **with the author preferring Barani and calling ʿIsami's
  "apocryphal" without giving a reason.**
- **Makhdum Jahaniyan's own rule on visits is broken by the Sultan one page earlier** — "Dervishes,
  however, should never allow such people to visit them at their Ahangahs [khanqahs]" (p. 302, folio 280)
  against "the Sultan … continued to visit him in his khangah [khanqah] throughout his stay" (p. 301,
  folio 279). **The book prints both and does not note the tension.**
- **He rules that renunciation after bequeathing property is fraudulent, and founds a hereditary line** —
  "it was fraudulent to adopt asceticism after bequeathing property to a son" (p. 300, folio 278) against
  the Uch succession passing brother-to-brother and then through "both the sons and grandsons" (pp. 303–304,
  folios 281–282).
- **He condemns lamps on graves; the book then records five saints' graves and a relic cult without
  comment** (p. 302, folio 280, against pp. 303, 305, 308, 314; folios 281, 283, 286, 292).
- **Bahaʾuʾd-Din Zakariyya's dislike of qalandars is stated absolutely and broken twice on the same page**
  (p. 328, folio 306).
- **The Chishti refusal of patronage against the Chishti acceptance of cash** — "Chishtis refused to accept
  land grants either from rulers or their officers" (p. 243, folio 223) and Baba Farid's "total rejection of
  government patronage" (p. 244, folio 224) against Nizamuʾd-Din accepting "enormous gifts given to him by
  Khusraw Barwar" and gifts "from princes like Khizr Khan" (p. 244, folio 224). **The book resolves it by a
  cash/land distinction but does not say Baba Farid held that distinction.**
- **Chishti abstention from politics asserted twice and contradicted three times** — "in general, Chishti
  sufis refrained from such activity" (p. 156, folio 138) and "Baba Farid was not associated with the
  political intrigues of his time" (p. 163, folio 145) against Shaikh Badruʾd-Din's khanqah from the
  Treasurer, Baba Farid accepting Ulugh Khan's cash, and Baba Farid interceding with the wali of Ajodhan
  (pp. 156, 162, 163; folios 138, 144, 145).
- **Borrowing is forbidden on the ground that his teachers permitted it, and his teacher gave it up**
  (pp. 155, 160; folios 137, 142).
- **Two men are called Qutbuʾd-Din Bakhtiyar Kaki's successor in Delhi, in the same chapter** (pp. 156, 158;
  folios 138, 140) — **and the book dissolves it by character rather than by evidence. A succession claim
  resting on four objects and a will, against a succession in fact: both belong in
  `shrine-of-fariduddin-ganjshakar`, neither alone.**
- **Where Baba Farid was initiated is disputed inside one paragraph** — Multan (Jamali) or Delhi (Rizvi,
  "more likely") (p. 157, folio 139).
- **Sufi income is single-source and multi-source within two pages** — "The unsolicited gift (futuh) from
  devotees was their only source of income" (p. 116, folio 98) against "there were two sources of income for
  a khanqah—endowments and begging" (p. 109, folio 91), against gifts "from rich men, government officials,
  princes and rulers" on the same page as the "only source" claim, and against "Many eminent sufis worked as
  petty traders, artisans or cultivators" (p. 117, folio 99).
- **Royal money strains khanqahs and restores them, three pages apart** (pp. 204, 207; folios 186, 189).
- **Aloofness from the state is asserted and contradicted** (p. 117, folio 99 against p. 104, folio 88; and
  pp. 203–207, folios 185–189).
- **The tomb cult is a degeneration and a normal institution on one page** — "Gradually the veneration of
  tombs degenerated into an excess of superstitious practices" eight lines below "this was often associated
  with a genuine feeling of respect for the Shaikh", and immediately above the ʿurs passage, which is given
  without criticism (p. 121, folio 103). **An entry quoting either sentence must carry the other.**
- **Temple demolition and religious tolerance five pages apart without comment** — "The Shaikh demolished
  the temple and constructed a takiya (khangah) [khanqah]" (p. 219, folio 201) against sufis "ready to
  appreciate the positive and true aspects contained in other religions" (p. 229, folio 209).
- **Guru Angad is called "the First Guru" while Arjan is "the fifth Guru"** (pp. 407, 419; folios 383, 395).
  **Either the book errs or "First" is an OCR substitution; recorded as printed and not to be entered as a
  fact.**
- **Guru Nanak's journey chronology does not close** — the Middle Eastern journey is placed after the 1528
  return, leaving 1528–1539 to hold a journey the book neither dates nor numbers (pp. 409–413, folios
  385–389).
- **Rizvi relies on McLeod and demolishes him in the same pages** (pp. 407, 409, 412, 413, 417; folios 383,
  385, 388, 389, 393). **The Gorakhtari identification that carries one of this file's bold ids rests on
  McLeod.**
- **Lifespans the book prints and does not remark on** — Shah Jalal at 150 lunar years (p. 337, folio 315);
  Shah Madar at 125 by the book's own arithmetic (pp. 340–341, folios 318–319); Muhammad Husaini bin Jaʿfar
  al-Makki at up to 164 (p. 264, folio 244).
- **Other unresolved pairs**: Amir Khusraw's two death dates (p. 190, folio 172); Amir Hasan's two (p. 191,
  folio 173); Nur Qutb-i ʿAlam's two, five years apart (p. 279, folio 259); Simnani's three, left genuinely
  open (p. 288, folio 268); Kabir's two, one of them rejected by the book (pp. 398–399, folios 374–375); the
  Khwaja's three death years and two birth years (pp. 136–137, folios 118–119); the *Padumavati*'s two
  composition dates, "947" and "927", **which differ by one OCR-plausible digit** (pp. 389, 394; folios 367,
  370); and the book's own cross-reference that points to the wrong printed page (p. 261, folio 241).
- **Three sentences that contradict themselves as printed and must not be quoted until checked** — the
  Jabariyya sentence (p. 39, folio 23); "Moreover he accused some Muslims of accepting what he saw as the
  higher status of prophets. To Hujwiri's dissatisfaction they advocated that the saints were superior to
  prophets." (p. 131, folio 113); and "This theory came to be later known as Wahdat al-Shuhud … and was
  distinct from the Wahdat al-Shuhud" (p. 269, folio 249). **Also "Some of the yogis who thronged the court
  of the Mongols were Buddhist Tantrics, but they were hardly distinguishable from the yogis" (p. 355,
  folio 333), where a word is probably lost.**

### 10. Apparatus, sigla and structural notes the coordinator needs

- **The sigla are never expanded anywhere in the chunks read.** `AA`, `SA`, `FF`, `KM`, `NU`, `Kalimat`,
  `A'in`, `KA` and the bare `Nicholson` carry no titles. **Consequence: most quotations in this book cannot
  be traced to a title and page from the pages they stand on.** One partial resolution is offered as
  inference and not as fact: the bare "Nicholson" is a Nicholson volume *of Hujwiri*, because the same
  siglum carries "Nicholson's preface, pp. IX—XII" and an index of "Hujwiri's Travels" (pp. 130–131, folios
  112–113), and is therefore **not** "R.A. Nicholson, The mystics of Islam, London, 1963", which the book
  cites separately and in full (p. 119, folio 101, footnote 2). **"NU" remains unexpanded.** The
  bibliography does not decode "Ethé" either: it lists "Sachau and Ethé, Catalogue of the Persian
  Manuscripts in the Bodleian Library, Oxford, 1889." (p. 455, folio 431) **while using "Bodleian" as a
  separate siglum from "Ethé, N", which means "Ethé" alone almost certainly points at a different catalogue
  that is nowhere listed.**
- **The book's own cross-references are to its printed pages, i.e. folios, not PDF pages** — "See the
  sections on Shaikh Abu Said bin Abi'l-Khair, pp. 68-69. supra, and Baba Farid, p. 139, supra" (p. 358,
  folio 336, footnote 6); "see pp. 86-8, supra" (p. 138, folio 120); "See pp. 199-200, injra [infra]"
  (p. 146, folio 128); "Baba Farid's instructions to Shaikh Nizamu'd-Din Auliya' in Chapter Two, p. 157-58,
  supra" (p. 409, folio 385). **A worker following "Baba Farid, p. 139" must look for folio 139, not PDF
  p. 139. One of them uses *supra* for a page that is later in the book (p. 173, folio 155).**
- **The brief's chapter ranges are stated without units and the book contradicts one reading.** "Chapter
  Two, 'The Chishtis' … opens at p. 132 and runs to p. 207": read as folios that is wrong, because **PDF
  p. 149 already prints the recto header `The Chishtis 131`** and PDF p. 148 (folio 130) continues the same
  narrative with no chapter division; read as PDF pages it is consistent. **The coordinator should fix the
  units before a worker is given the chapter's last chunk, where the two readings differ by twenty-eight
  pages.** The book nowhere prints "Chapter Two" in pp. 148–163.
- **Pages that print no folio, so `(p. N, folio not stated)` is correct and no folio has been computed** —
  pp. 4, 31, 32, 34, 221, 222, 298, 392, 421, 425, 428, 433, 436, 457, and the Bibliography opening at
  p. 437. **p. 436 carries nothing but the scan colophon; Appendix C completes cleanly on p. 435 (folio 411)
  and the Bibliography opens cleanly on p. 437, so it is most likely a blank verso, but a human should check
  the physical page.** **p. 457 should print folio 433 by the pattern of its neighbours and does not.**
  **p. 91's running header prints `Early Sufism 77`, out of sequence between 74 and 76; it has been treated
  as OCR-damaged (5→7) and cited as folio 75, marked "corrected" — the one page in the book where the
  printed header does not win, and the reason is damage, not dissent.** **p. 159 prints `The Chishtis 14]`
  and p. 209 prints `…Silsilas 19]`, both with the final digit broken; the folio map's values were used and
  nothing was computed.**
- **Three dates in the book's own front matter do not agree** — "First published 1978" and "©1975" on one
  page (p. 4, folio not stated), with the Acknowledgements dated "1 January, 1978." (p. 11, folio not
  stated).
- **Three AH/AD pairs cannot stand as printed and are the highest-priority locator checks**: "15 Muharram
  710/20 September 1301" (p. 18, folio 4); "Qut al-Qulub, Cairo, I, 1310/1891-93" (p. 45, folio 29); and
  Hallaj's imprisonment arithmetic, which works in CE but not in hijri — 301/913 to 309/1 April 922, with
  "Imprisoned for nine years" (pp. 73–74, folios 57–58). **All recorded exactly as printed and none
  corrected.** **Every other AH/CE pair in this book is unverified: a tesseract digit swap leaves no
  trace.**
- **Volume I stops "to 1600 AD" (p. 3, folio not stated)** and the author reserves the Qadiri, Naqshbandi,
  Shattari and later Chishti material for Volume II (pp. 15–16). **A large share of the archive's rows —
  every Qadiri, Naqshbandi and Suhrawardi row of the seventeenth century and later, and every
  Nanakpanthi/Udasi, Sevapanthi and Sant row — cannot be served from this volume at all**, and none of them
  is touched anywhere in the 28 chunks (not stated).
